Live-DTB check: identical (sha 947ec62d; capability=0x03 is a runtime fixup by the vendor driver, not a flash delta). | 2026-11-22 |  | 2026-11-22 17:05 - *** BREAKTHROUGH: PCIe LINK TRAINS ON THE 6.18 IMAGE. Root cause was NEVER the PCIe register program - it was BOARD GPIO state. *** Method: built a standalone loadable 6.18 module (luofu_standalone.ko) replicating the vendor hi_pcie.ko sequence with NO DWC framework; it first failed too (link LTSSM 0x00). Then bisected GPIO0 controller state: stock (vendor kernel) drives GPIO0 DDR=0x1330000f / DATA=0x12300004; our image drives DDR=0x0300000f / DATA=0x0300000f. Writing the STOCK values to 0x10106000/0x10106004 and re-running the standalone module: LTSSM=0x11 (L0), DEBUG0=0x03d15111, DEBUG1=0x08000110, and config read cfg[0]=0x000559e7 (the Wi-Fi endpoint answered). Bisect results: DATA bits 0/1/3/24 high are harmless; setting DATA to ours (clearing bits 25/28) DROPS the link; restoring recovers it automatically. DDR: pin21 as output is REQUIRED, pin28 as output is REQUIRED, pin20 output is not. Thus GPIO0 pin28 must be OUTPUT HIGH (PERST deassert / power) and pin21 OUTPUT LOW - the Wi-Fi endpoint's power/reset controls on this board. Our DTS has no node for 20/21/28 (stock kernel configures them). FIX applied in pcie-luofu driver host_init: ioremap 0x10106000, DDR \|= BIT(21)\|BIT(28), DATA = (DATA & ~BIT(21)) \| BIT(28)  -> W25a build. Endpoint config space read live from working link: vendor 0x59e7 device 0x0005, class 0x02800000 (network controller), bars 4/4/4, rev 0xe5. | `mem-entries.md:27398` |
| 2026-11-22 |  | 2026-11-22 17:20 - GPIO root cause pinned down EXACTLY (hardware-verified by runtime bisect on W25a/W25b): the Wi-Fi endpoint (Hi5622/TR5220) needs GPIO0 pins 20, 21 and 28 to be OUTPUTS driven HIGH before PCIe training. Stock (vendor kernel) GPIO0 state: DDR=0x1330000f, DATA=0x12300004 -> output pins 0,1,2,3,20,21,24,25,28 with 20,21,28 all high. Our image (DTS gpio-leds on 0,1,2,3,24,25, no node for 20/21/28) left 20/21/28 as inputs and drove 0,1,3,24 high (LEDs off, active-low). Bisect results: (a) DATA=0x1330000f + DDR=0x1330000f (LEDs-high plus 20/21/28 high) -> LTSSM=0x11 L0, cfg=0x000559e7; (b) DATA=0x12300004 + same DDR -> also trains; (c) any of bits 20/21/28 low (data or direction) -> no link. So fix = ddr \|= BIT(20)\|BIT(21)\|BIT(28) and data \|= BIT(20)\|BIT(21)\|BIT(28) in host_init (plus msleep(1000) settle because our probe is at ~2.5 s while the vendor module runs ~11 s). W25d is the image with this exact fix. | `mem-entries.md:27402` |

Then a 600-iteration race loop holding gate=0xcecc while continuously clearing out[1] (+ack/rearm writes): NO out[1] bit6/0x8, NO DR fill advance (stays 0x10), NO group-4 frame. The mixed plausible-pointer/random words in a region that the populator would memset indicate STALE/UNINITIALIZED device SRAM - consistent with the boot task never having run its init blocks in this chip power-cycle. Hypothesis: our SoC 'reboot -f' warm reboots do NOT power-cycle the Wi-Fi chip, so we have been experimenting on a chip whose SRAM state is stale/half-initialized; the vendor path expects a clean ROM->boot sequence after cold power-on. ACTION: request a physical power-cycle from the user, then rerun the clean sequence (B1 + P0 + gate) on a cold chip and re-read registry/state. | `mem-entries.md:27450` |
| 2026-11-22 |  | 2026-11-22 21:20 - INCIDENT + LESSONS: the user's cold power-cycle ran the TFTP recovery (server was still armed) and restored stock over BOTH pairs; our W27b image on pair B is gone. Recovery semantics (loader-re SUMMARY, verified): a RESET-hold POR TFTPs recovery.bin (vendor 2.4.15 image, RSA-2048 gate - custom recovery images impossible without the vendor key), writes kernela AND kernelb, rootfsa AND rootfsb with the same image and ERASES rootfs_data. The recovered stock boots into the first-boot LuCI wizard, whose "Create an administrator password" step refuses with "Invalid board info!" (bdinfo not readable in this state) so no admin password can be set; the vendor's MQTT/msg-center client is consequently not provisioning, so the root broker takeover (tools/box-provision.sh / mqtt_takeover.py) has no device client to talk to, SSH/telnet are absent, and there is no command channel left. The box is otherwise reachable (LAN 192.168.10.1, web UI served) and pair A/B both hold stock; U-Boot env/cell helpers: env bootflag selects the pair, SYSCTRL 0x10100C08 is the failure record, true POR clears the cell (re-confirmed). LESSONS: (1) NEVER leave the TFTP recovery server armed when a user power-cycle is planned - kill tools/tftp_server.py first; (2) a recovery image is the only thing the TFTP path accepts (RSA); (3) after any recovery the stock overlay is empty and the device needs cloud/internet provisioning before the broker channel exists. NEXT PATH: get the stock provisioned (connect the router WAN to an internet-facing network and power-cycle so the vendor client can complete first-boot provisioning), then the broker takeover works again and we re-flash W27b to pair B via the known-good flashimg flow; alternatives considered: custom recovery image (RSA-blocked), in-brief image boot window (too short to flash), LuCI wizard automation (blocked on invalid bdinfo). | `mem-entries.md:27454` |

| 2026-11-23 |  | 2026-11-23 12:15 - C1 gate-hold test: NEGATIVE, plus a better park-point localisation. Attended session on the recovered box: host warm reboot (B3 rule: no rmmod after release), B3 loaded poll_ms=0, CHIP state before iATU = 0xffff (window not decoding), prepare 6/6 OK, upload 928,920 B, verify diffs=0, P0 arm 7/7 (pre-release DR fill 0x10), release (dmesg CPU release; the release word CA 0x40000108 reads 0x00000000 afterwards = the CPU consumed 0x5a5a). Then sustained gate service (CA 0x4000010C <- 0xcece, ~2,000,000 writes over ~2 min, one per ~100 us) instead of a single write. RESULTS: gate reads 0x0000cece during service (writes land), BUT out[1]=0x00000004 (bit2 only), mask=0x08, g=0, state=1, out[0]/out[2]/out[5]=0 - identical to the single-write park. KEY INSIGHT from the firmware disassembly check: the entry routine sets out[1] = 4 (file 0x86F22-0x86F2C), writes the D2H doorbell (0x86F54-0x86F66), then does out[1] \|= 8 (0x86F68-0x86F70) and ONLY THEN spins on CA 0x4000010C == 0xcece (0x86F74-0x86F7C; literal 0x4000010C at 0x86FA0). We never see bit3 (0x8) in out[1] (it stays 0x4), so the CPU is NOT parked at the gate spin - it parks earlier, most likely inside the calls at 0x86F3E-0x86F50 (bl to 0xfff799c0/0xfff7998e) or the entry-configure loop at 0x86F04-0x86F20. So the C1 hypothesis (sustained 0xcece) is DISPROVEN as the missing action: the spin is never reached. Also confirmed the lane's claim that no vendor host module writes 0xcece (negative control) - the gate value must come from elsewhere/earlier, or the spin is not the blocker at all. NEXT: have boot-kick-re re-analyse where entry 3 actually parks (disassemble 0x86E14-0x86F78 fully, resolve the bl targets, check for hardware waits), and propose the next single-candidate session (likely the L1SS pinmux C2 writes CA 0x40000554/0x400005BC, which our transport never writes). VENDOR PCI: two PCI domains, both the same device (vendor 0x59e7 device 0x0005) and BOTH map the SAME chip (identical state via 0x40000000 and 0x58000000): domain 0000:00 BAR0 0x40000000 16MB / BAR2 0x41800000 16KB / BAR4 0x41000000 8MB; domain 0001:00 BAR0 0x58000000 / BAR2 0x59800000 / BAR4 0x59000000; driver rox_pci0 bound to both. Vendor iATU viewports (BAR2 read): vp0 base 0x40000000 lim 0x401BFFFF tgt 0; vp1 0x401C0000-0x401D7FFF tgt 0x400000; vp2 0x401D8000-0x403B7FFF tgt 0x1000000; vp3 0x403B8000-0x404D7FFF tgt 0x40000000; vp4 0x404D8000-0x406B7FFF tgt 0x2000000; vp5 0x406B8000-0x408CFFFF tgt 0x1200000; vp6/vp7 off => the vendor RC maps BAR0 at ABSOLUTE bus 0x40000000 (identity RC translation), while our transport uses BAR0-relative viewports (our RC translates CPU 0x40000000 -> PCI 0x0); both reach the same chip. Vendor driver init (dmesg): hi_pcie RC mode 11.8s; six inbound regions per controller (0x58000000/0x581C0000/0x581D8000/0x583B8000/0x584D8000/0x586B8000, sizes 0x1C0000/0x18000/0x1E0000/0x120000/0x1E0000/0x218000); BAR2 dbi 0x59800000 16KB; BAR4 0x59000000 8MB; outbound target 0x80000000 (host DDR); pcie_acp OFF for 10160000/10164000, pie_acp ON for 10a70000; firmware ini /lib/firmware/hi_wifi/cfg_hi5622v100_hisi.ini loaded; HOST HCC STACK: group::0 hcc_port_id 0/1/2 -> bal_port_id 0/3/6; threads "HCC TX Thread" (bindcpu 0x1) and "Host PLAT RX" (bindcpu 0x255); pcie_init_chip_res / pcie_main_init chip[0] bus[0]. KEY INTERPRETATION: the chip firmware's boot state on the working vendor host is the same as ours, so the missing input is in how the host DRIVES the chip after boot: the vendor runs its full HCC/BAL host stack (HCC TX/SRX threads + mailbox servicing) whereas our transport uses its own service loop (continuous 0xcece gate writes + drain writes), and the vendor host NEVER writes 0xcece (host-replica lane). Our chip's mask 0x0A (svc+data abort) vs vendor 0 suggests our servicing drives the firmware into its fault. NEXT EXPERIMENT (bench): boot our transport but service D2H in vendor style (no 0xcece, proper out[1] event ack/drain per vendor pcie_msg_handle; maybe also no announce loop) and check whether mask stays 0 and the ready path (g4 id1/id2) runs. Also capture vendor host-side register writes via kprobes if the stock kernel allows. Golden raw logs were not saved to host (telnet capture files landed on stock only), but the values above were transcribed; box is back on OUR image (pair B, 6.18.60, SSH ok). | `mem-entries.md:27542` |

| 2026-11-23 |  | 2026-11-23 19:20 - ROOT-CAUSE HYPOTHESIS FOUND: out[1] bit6 (0x40) = pcie_trigger_ete_sending (firmware asks the host to send ETE/HCC frames; vendor handler pcie_trigger_ete_sending_handle 0x15efc -> pcie_wkup_thread wakes the TX kthread), and out[1] bit2 (0x04) = exception_panic_msg_process (firmware PANIC). Observed timeline on our chip after release: t0.2s out1=0x40 (trigger ETE sending) -> t1.2s out1=0x04 (PANIC) with mask 0x08. Our service loop only DRAINS the bitmap (out[3]=1/out[1]=0/out[4]=1) and never dispatches the per-bit handlers, so the firmware's ETE-send request is ignored, the 1s wait expires and the firmware panics (exception path) => the SVC and the later data abort. The vendor host responds to bit6 by waking its TX thread and sending the ETE frames; its chip never panics (mask=0 golden). CORRECT vendor D2H order (host-replica 4.2): ack *out[3]=1 FIRST, then read bitmap out[1], drain out[1]=0, re-arm out[4]=1, then dispatch bits (id = ctz) with table handlers for ids 1,2,3,4,5,6,7 (1=dev-ready stub, 2=panic, 3=ETE transfer done -> drain DR channels 0x1f, 4/5=PM, 6/7=trigger ETE sending -> wake TX). NEXT: capture the firmware's DR-ring messages (the transport's pciet_d2h_payload copies wire messages into read_msg[core], u16 len at +4, core byte at +9, payload at +0xc) so we can see exactly what the firmware asked for, then implement the bit6 handler = actually send the ETE/HCC frames our transport already has an SR ring for, and re-test whether the firmware avoids the panic (mask stays 0) and the ready exchange (g4 id1/id2) begins. | `mem-entries.md:27550` |

NEXT TEST GP6: delay the endpoint reset pulse in the RC driver to match the vendor timing window (e.g., probe at ~2.6 s but wait ~8-10 s before pulsing, or move the pulse after the clock/LTSSM setup at a later point), rebuild+flash, re-run GP5 writes with the delayed pulse, and check +8=2. S0 = caches/MPU off, per-mode stacks (0x1BF110/0x1BEF00), CP15 c6 MPU-style regions, MPU on, MPIDR split CPU0 primary/CPU1 secondary. Dispatch: CPU0 cold fallback writes 0xBAAD to CA 0x4000010C, analog init, polls [0x40000108]==0x5A5A (host release), writes **0xCEED** to 16-bit CA 0x4000010C, sets page bit8, re-reads [0x40000], blx entry A 0x46970. **CPU1's S1 (mask ROM 0x5FE) writes handshake +8=2 then blx entry B 0xC742C**, after a WFI loop that passes only when [0x1BF00C]==0xABCD AND 16-bit [0x4000010C]==0xCEED. The image never writes 2; entry B is CPU1-dispatched after S1. **EXACT GATE: our transport writes 0xCECE to CA 0x4000010C (BAR0 0x3B810C) as the D2H announce gate and re-writes it continuously, clobbering the ROM's 0xCEED written by the CPU0 fallback at the 0x5A5A release. One 16-bit register carries two mutually exclusive handshakes: ROM boot stage CEED vs image service/exception CECE (image 0xC20F8/0xC6E14 spin for CECE). The vendor never writes CECE and its mask=0, so CEED survives -> golden +8=2.** HOST ACTION (subtractive): release 0x00005A5A -> CA 0x40000108 only; DO NOT write 0xCECE during boot (only after page+8==2 / page+0 bit14 set); optional repair if +8 stays 0: read 16-bit 0x4000010C and if != CEED write 0x0000CEED (CPU1-only consumer). Expected: page 0x86807FEF / 0x7 / 0x2 / 0xABCD; state CA 0x118B60=1; mask 0. Corrections: image header order = {FIQ, data-abort, prefetch-abort, SVC, undefined}; r1 ids undef=1, svc=2, prefetch=3, abort=4 => bench mask 0x0A = undefined+prefetch (NOT svc+data-abort); 0x7386F44F is Thumb code not a magic; 0x1F4/0x1F8 are stack tops; IRQ vector points into image 0x40064. Also explains earlier no-gate-service sessions reaching state=1 (when no CECE clobber). NEXT: GP8 bench - single clean pulse, GP4/GP5 pre-seeding (both custom blocks), upload/verify, release-only with NO 0xCECE writes, read page at 0.2/1/5/20 s + state/mask; repair with CEED if needed. | `mem-entries.md:27650` |

| 2026-11-25 |  | 2026-11-25 21:30 - H1/H2 DECISIVE PACK RESULT: H1 CONFIRMED - PD4's stub is CONSUME-ONLY (the live handler table is EMPTY). Read the 5-word pack with the proven physdump path on a PD4 golden boot (2 s post-release, verified again at 48 s): **CPU 0x10C1B0 (instance+0x20, handler-table pointer) = 0x00118D68 = the image's static value (file 0xCC1B0 contains 0x00118D68 exactly once)**; **the live table at CPU 0x118D68 is ALL ZERO (entries id1/id3/id5 = 0) and stays zero at 48 s** (the boot memset zeroed it and the mailbox registration helper never populated it this session); ROM RAZ window 0x2000 = 0; sanity anchor CPU 0x40000 = 0x00046971 (image loaded correctly). Instance words around it: +0x10 CA 0x10C1A0 = 0x400392D4 (doorbell), +0x14 = 0x40101418, +0x18/+0x1C = 0, +0x20 = 0x00118D68, +0x24 = 0x00360036, +0x28 = 0x001043EA, +0x2C = 0xDEAD4EAD. INTERPRETATION: the PD4 stub's sentinel compare is ALWAYS EQUAL -> it always substitutes the ROM RAZ zero table for the dispatcher call -> **the firmware's mailbox dispatcher handlers are NEVER executed under PD4**; only the ack/clear/doorbell side effects run (and the ROM zeros make the cbz NULL handler checks safe). This REFUTES the earlier assumption (in several lane reports) that PD4 dispatches through the real vendor handler table: the DUPLEX SUCCESS (calibration answers, ctx+0x190 allocation) therefore happens through a NON-MAILBOX path - consistent with the ETE SR engine delivering our H2D frames into device memory and the firmware's pcie_msg_init polled H2D thread processing them (the mailbox ack is bookkeeping). PD4-NOSENT would crash with an empty table; PD4-ALWAYS0 equals the current behavior. This is a citable correction for the RE report (fw-audit H1/H2 answered; re-report2's 6.6 section needs updating). ALSO: bench2 fixed the phase-matrix gate bug (opensource 5d93b4d): the generated gate helpers grepped a hard-coded repo path instead of the passed pattern (template edit artifact) so EVERY attr/output gate failed; now helpers use $p + whitespace normalization, host evaluator _search_pattern_ws, regression tests (selftest 398/398), C2 dry-run PLAN-OK; replay of the failed fire passes load-budget. (2) REAL BUG in the v7 base latch: 'static bool hi5622_vap_base_posted' is MODULE-GLOBAL and never cleared on del_virtual_intf (even though del posts firmware user_del g0 id9); delete+re-add the VAP between scan and AP start (or bind/unbind within one load) -> start_ap skips the VAP base for a firmware VAP object that no longer exists -> AP frames hit a dead/NULL object (the v6 mask-0x18 crash class). FIX: per-ctx latch cleared on user_del success and on probe. (3) mgmt_tx instrumentation gating (mgmt_diag param) in the hardening patch. MY 21:30 REPRO CONTEXT: that boot had NO earlier AP-ENABLED (first hostapd attempt), the iface WAS deleted and re-added as __ap, and the module was loaded with vap_start_first=1 (the scan posted the mode-1 VAP base) -> the -EALREADY hypothesis cannot explain it; hypothesis 2 (stale module-global latch) is the match. LAUNCHED the requested A-D boots (single background run, fresh golden boot each, no in-boot retry): A first-AP-after-scan, B restart control (no scan), C AP->scan->killall->restart, D stale-latch test (managed scan -> del -> __ap); capturing the dmesg slices (start_ap reached? frame-post REFUSED/lane-closed) + boot page/mask per boot. Both root causes are now hardware-confirmed; the stability lane's hardening patch (per-ctx latch cleared on user_del/probe, fail-closed stop_ap/restart policy, mgmt_diag) addresses both. Boots C/D were running at record time. | 2026-11-28 |  | 2026-11-28 16:00 - STATIC-RE: **wifi-rx-static lane complete** (opensource 5215bcf, docs/incoming/wifi-rx-static/) - THE RX MYSTERY IS SOLVED: **we were polling the wrong block**. Live MAC ring pointers are machw word 8 = **CA 0x40050000**: offset 0x00 status, 0x08 norm rptr, 0x0c small rptr, 0x10 cmp wptr. The config block +0x4c/+0x50/+0x54 (CA 0x40040000) are NEVER read by the vendor and stay 0 (stock live dump: 0x40050008/0c = 0x20 vs config 0). So all our prior dp_rx_poll reads of cmp/norm/small rptrs were reading dead configuration registers - that is why RX "never moved". Also decoded: completion entry = devVA(buffer+4); the device header is 36 B at buffer+4 (len at +0x1a -> buf+0x1e, next at +0x20 -> buf+0x24, MPDU at buf+0x28; vendor len+0x28 <= 0x640) - the inherited decoder was 4 B off; and **the 14-write program IS the whole device enable** (the free-ring producer publish gates MAC prefetch; no extra rx-enable bit/H2D message) - the missing half was the CONSUMER (ring rptr advance/refill). MODULE/PATCH: patches/0001-rx-live-enable.patch (6 files) adds portable hi5622_rx_live.c/h + glue: rx_enable=0 fail-closed; read-only dp_rx_arm (live-bank validation = the L1 proof); pointer-moving dp_rx_drain (bounded consume, header/chain/entry validation, refill <= live rptr delta, publish wptr/rptr, lane closes on any anomaly); live telemetry in dp_rx_poll/dp_rx_capture; writes only config +0x38/3c/40 (+0x44 only if rx_clear_status=1); live bank read-only; no IRQ/firmware message. Host selftest 51/51; kernel build RC=0 0 warnings; patch round-trips byte-for-byte. Fireable artifact: /tmp/wifi-rx-lane-artifact/hi5622_wifi.ko 280,736 B sha256 5b43d0a2... (unstripped 1,021,976 B 3649f589...). Decoded the g0 id10 handler CA 0x5FF64 completely: payload[0]=3, payload[1]=1\|2, object via 0xD6A1E, **per-slot contexts at obj+0xEC+slot*0x20 (8 slots x 0x20 B)**; the scan engine ([[module+0x4B8]+0x1A00]) **consumes slot 0 ONLY** (+0xFC start, +0xFD step, +0xFE end/count, +0x103 mode, +0x104/105 result counters, +0x106 stride default 6), and **no subtype-1 field writes +0xFE** - only the subtype-2 clear path writes it (to 0, CA 0x5FEE0) - so more slot configs cannot widen one 13-entry request; the 7-8 channel ceiling is STRUCTURAL. Corrections to the earlier H1 decode: dispatch row is CA 0x10CD5C (not 0x10CD08); the message is the vendor's ADDBA-rsp/DELBA relay with a **44 B wire shape** (32 B payload, not R4's 36 B) and the subtype-2 flag is payload+0x14 (not +0x08); the R4 H1 effect is re-explained as PACING: payload+0x10 presets ctx+0x104 (the result counter), payload+0x12 sets mode. DELIVERABLES: patches/0001-scan-full-vendor-shape-bandcfg.patch (applies cleanly on R4 + hardened trees), refined src/hi5622_band_cfg.{c,h} (vendor-shaped 44 B, corrected map, fail-closed), tools/fw_scan_full_re.py (65/65 static checks regenerating every CA), TEST-LADDER-R11.md (pacing sweep + slot-inert falsifier); host selftest 90/90, 6.18.60 build 0 warnings (local stripped 263,704 B sha 4dd008d8, 10 imports); scan-tuning 37/37 and scan-complete 196/196 still green. RECOMMENDATION (adopted): close H1, keep scan_band_cfg=0 as the shipped default. HONEST SCAN CEILING FINAL: 6 channels/request without H1 (batching single-shot, cannot re-arm), 7-8 with the H1 pacing config (documented, off by default), full 13 structurally impossible on this firmware (slot 0 only). This is a clean, evidence-backed negative result that closes the scan line. Remaining static lanes: beacon-static (task-140), sta-static (task-141). Soak on the final R4 combined module continues (golden:1 hostapd=4). | `mem-entries.md:28074` |

| 2026-11-25 |  | 2026-11-25 12:00 - PUBLIC-RESEARCH-2 LANE DELIVERED (opensource 864e606, docs/incoming/wifi-public-research-2/) - THREE HIGH-VALUE FINDINGS + a corrected next-try plan. (1) **THE MGMT-TX CARRIER WE IMPLEMENTED IS THE WRONG MESSAGE**: the 0x01F2 WAL cfg-event is a host-internal WAL->HMAC request, NOT a device message; the sibling stack (Hi3881 2021 + Hi1105 Wi-Fi7) maps WLAN_CFGID_CFG80211_MGMT_TX -> hmac_event.c:289 in-host hmac_wpas_mgmt_tx -> the frame is sent as a FRW_EVENT_TYPE_WLAN_CTX / DMAC_WLAN_CTX_EVENT_SUB_TYPE_MGMT event (hmac_mgmt_bss_comm.c:1453) wrapped by HCC into a control frame with extend header {nest_type=WLAN_CTX, nest_sub_type=MGMT, vap_id, config_frame=0} (hcc_hmac.c:97/438/540); completion = a CRX mgmt-tx-status event (hmac_event.c:437). **CORROBORATED IN OUR OWN VENDOR DISASSEMBLY: luofu hmac_wpas_mgmt_tx @0x4050c -> helper @0x3fe68 does raw hcc_msg_alloc/hcc_msg_tx with mgmt sub-type byte 6, NOT the WAL TLV. The PD4 table-A absence of 0x01F2 is expected, not a blocker.** => the ap-mgmt M2 ladder as written must not be fired; the .mgmt_tx path needs retargeting to the DMAC WLAN_CTX/MGMT event (sibling-rx lane re-tasked to decode the luofu path @0x4050c/0x3fe68 + the extend header + completion). (2) **MANAGEMENT RX IS A CRX EVENT (our g1 id13), not data-ring traffic**: probe/auth/assoc frames arrive as DMAC->host mgmt events and are forwarded to cfg80211; the MAC RX ring staying 0 during a client scan is EXPECTED; the ring is for EAPOL/data after association. (3) **THE DMAC RX FILTER IS A MISSING HOST STEP**: the sibling posts a SET_RX_FILTER event at VAP state changes: AP-UP single-VAP = 0xF7B9FAEA, AP+STA-up = 0x73B9FACA, 0 = keep; BIT4 = broadcast management (probe req) - if our AP start never programs it, the firmware may forward no mgmt frames: a cheap next-try before doubting the decoder. (4) LINUX RX CROSS-CHECK: publishing the free-buffer producer IS the RX enable; completion entries carry buffer addresses; host consumes->refills->republishes at a watermark (iwlwifi RX_STALLED) - supports rx-static's "consumer was the missing half"; add a refill watermark before sustained EAPOL. (5) SCAN: the sibling host sends the whole channel list in one request (no host cap) -> our 6->7/8 ceiling is DMAC-side, not publicly work-around-able; the public request struct is the field dictionary for our 520-B g0 id0xC (H1 field10->count fits a count/dwell field). (6) SOURCE HUNT: no public exact-chip source/datasheet/SDK (OpenHarmony has only hi3881v100; the Cudy WR3000 2.0 GPL section is present but EMPTY - verified raw HTML - while v1 has a real GPL row; no Jeton GPL; no mainline Linux HiSilicon Wi-Fi driver). (7) LICENSING: our opensource is MIT vs Hi3881 GPL-2.0 -> facts/structures only, no verbatim copying. Verification: 51/51 sources re-downloaded at commit-pinned URLs (byte+sha match). RANKED NEXT: T1 luofu mgmt-TX decode -> implement+fire; T2 RX filter; T3 mgmt-RX fire with an external client; T4 EAPOL ring watermark; T5 scan field mapping; T6 STA-join. The sibling-rx lane (task-151) is re-tasked with T1+T2. The R11 channel sweep so far: field10 29/33/41/64 => 7/6/6/6 channels (best remains 8 at field10=25); W5/W6 pending. | `mem-entries.md:28146` |

NEXT SESSION: focus entirely on the MAC RX capture - compare the vendor's stock register/ring state against ours (a vendor-dump vs ours diff, the rx-static lane's L1-L5 rung detail), the ring entry/buffer write-back format, and the firmware's CRX event gating. | 2026-11-26 |  | 2026-11-26 00:00 - RX-CAPTURE SWARM LAUNCHED (5 lanes: rx-vendor-diff, rx-format, rx-crx-gating, rx-capture-plan, rx-linux-parity) + a CRITICAL BENCH FINDING: **THE FIRMWARE CRASHES MID-SESSION**. Current bench state: router up, module loaded, AP-ENABLED wr3000v2-apstart; the page: 0x86807fef/7/2/0xabcd with **golden:0**, mask CA 0x400008 = **0x04000010**, hcc_events **excp_announce=1 vector_mask=0x00000010** (d2h words=45: id2=1, id6=44 heartbeats). Earlier 0x04000010 occurrences were the E2/stale-VAP crash class, but the AP started cleanly this time - so another trigger exists. PRIME SUSPECT: **our dp_rx_drain / watermark refill writes** (the ring pointer/descriptor writes; the crash appeared after extended drain loops) - the laptop-sent auth frames never appear (no g1 id13, the live bank rptr stays 0x20) so (A) the RX capture path is missing something even before any crash, AND (B) the firmware eventually faults. | 2026-11-26 |  | 2026-11-26 22:00 - *** 5GHz REGDOMAIN BLOCKER SOLVED (a real image fix) ***. ROOT CAUSE: the kernel was built with CONFIG_CFG80211_REQUIRE_SIGNED_REGDB=y (hidden behind CFG80211_CERTIFICATION_ONUS) and the shipped regdb (regulatory.db 6332/6348 B) lacked its signature file (regulatory.db.p7s) - and even with the matched signed pair copied from the host, the kernel's pkcs7 verify failed ('loaded regulatory.db is malformed or signature is missing/invalid') -> the world regdomain -> 5GHz NO-IR -> the 5GHz AP refused. FIX (host build + a targeted module swap, no flash): (1) added CONFIG_EXPERT=y + CONFIG_CFG80211_CERTIFICATION_ONUS=y + # CONFIG_CFG80211_REQUIRE_SIGNED_REGDB is not set to the kernel .config (the build_dir's + the target's config-6.18 - NOTE: the option is hidden behind ONUS which depends on EXPERT, so both must be set or olddefconfig re-enables the default y); (2) rebuilt ONLY net/wireless/cfg80211.ko (2317952 B, vermagic 6.18.60, 0 pkcs7 refs) and deployed it to /lib/modules/6.18.60/cfg80211.ko on the box (the original backed up as .orig.bak); (3) rmmod/modprobe cfg80211; (4) iw reg set US -> 'country US: DFS-FCC' + the 5GHz band '5150-5250 @ 80, 23 dBm, AUTO-BW' (IR, no PASSIVE-SCAN) = THE 5GHZ AP IS NOW ALLOWED. ALSO: the signed regdb pair (host's regulatory.db 6348 B + .p7s 1085 B) copied to /lib/firmware (for completeness). THE 5GHZ AP ATTEMPT (same boot, after the reg fix): the start_ap reached the driver (beacon_interval=100 dtim=2 head=63 tail=32 - the 5GHz beacon!) + the 5-step plan, but the vap_add was REFUSED (H2D lane closed by a previous timeout) -> the firmware exception (mask 0x12, page golden:0). The likely cause: the recipe's order (dp_setup BEFORE the AP) vs the PROVEN 2.4GHz order (AP FIRST, then dp_setup). NEXT: power-cycle + retry with the AP-first order (module -> ladder -> C2 -> 5GHz AP ch36 -> the observer scan 5180; the dp_setup only after if needed for the data path). The regdomain fix is permanent (the cfg80211.ko + the config committed to the build; the image bake should include it). The internet stayed via the USB tether throughout. | `mem-entries.md:28304` |
